THIRD-PARTY LICENCES FOR THE ARWEN BRIDGE BUNDLE
==============================================================================

This file is the binary-form notice for the compiled artefacts distributed
in this bundle and staged into ArWen platform wheels: 27 Rust executables and
libraries, plus the map assets beside it and two embedded fonts.

Those binaries are built from two kinds of source, and both carry third-party
licence conditions.  The first is the vendored dependency trees --
section 3 inventories the original bridge trees and section 4 reproduces
their texts; section 5 covers the locked terminal workspace dependencies
from tools/arwen-ui-vendor.  The second
is ArWen's OWN crates, several of which port third-party code into
themselves: the Py-ART dealiaser, the MPAS initialisation and static chain
behind the five rw_mpas_* binaries, and the NumPy float32 kernels inside
static_fields.  A first-party crate carrying third-party expression belongs
to no dependency tree, which is exactly why MPAS and NumPy went unnotified
until the 2.6.6 development lane, included in 2.7.0. Section 2 covers
that second kind, and it is maintained by
hand; the regeneration recipe below rebuilds sections 3 and 4 only.

It is here because the licences below require it.  BSD-3-Clause clause 2,
Apache-2.0 section 4, MIT, ISC, Zlib, the Unicode licence and SIL OFL 1.1
clause 2 each condition redistribution IN BINARY FORM on the copyright notice
and licence text accompanying the distribution "in the documentation and/or
other materials provided with the distribution".  A zip of stripped binaries
with no licence text performs none of that.  This file is that performance.

WHERE THIS FILE LIVES, AND WHY THERE ARE TWO COPIES
---------------------------------------------------
The binaries reach users by two routes, and the notice has to travel on
both.

  * GitHub release bridge bundles.  The copy at
    tools/rustwx/assets/basemap/THIRD-PARTY-LICENSES.txt is packed into
    every one of them, because
    tools/build_bridge_bundle.py::collect_assets() carries every regular
    file under each of bridge_assets.REQUIRED_ASSET_SUBDIRS, which today is
    ("basemap",).  Placing it there is what makes it travel with the
    binaries without a change to the packing code.  Its scope is the whole
    bundle, not the map data; the map data's own provenance is in the
    README.md beside each layer and in upstream-lock.json.

  * Platform wheels, which stage the same binaries into
    gpuwm/libexec/bridges/ and do NOT carry assets/.  The copy at
    licenses/THIRD-PARTY-LICENSES-bridge-binaries.txt reaches those through
    pyproject.toml's license-files, landing at
    gpuwm-<version>.dist-info/licenses/licenses/.

The two copies are generated together and are byte-identical.

HOW TO REGENERATE IT
--------------------
Walk tools/rustwx/vendor, tools/grib1_bridge/vendor and tools/rw_wps/vendor
for every directory holding a Cargo.toml; read its name, version and license
expression; collect every LICENSE*/COPYING* file in that directory;
deduplicate the texts by SHA-256, keeping every distinct one so that no
copyright line is lost; then emit the inventory and the texts.  The counts in
section 3 are what that walk returns on this tree.  That walk does NOT
produce section 2: those notices belong to code ArWen wrote by porting, not
to a vendored crate, so nothing in the vendor trees names them.  Section 2 is
edited by hand and gated by tests/test_licence_notices_ship.py.

The TUI's additional fourth vendor tree is resolved for the supported Linux
and Windows targets by `python tools/update_tui_license_notice.py`. This
refreshes section 5 in both copies while preserving sections 1 through 4.
Pass --check to verify that both copies match the current Cargo inputs and
vendored licence/attribution texts without changing either file.

1.  THE BUNDLE'S OWN CODE
------------------------------------------------------------------------------

ArWen is Copyright 2026 ArWen contributors and is licensed under the Apache
License, Version 2.0; the full text is in the LICENSE file of the source
repository, and the repository NOTICE records every third-party grant in the
project, including those that reach only the source distribution.

The tools/rustwx and tools/rw_wps workspaces, from which most of the binaries
in this bundle are built, declare license = "MIT" in their own Cargo.toml and
carry that text at tools/rustwx/LICENSE.  tools/grib1_bridge declares no
licence of its own and takes the repository's Apache-2.0 grant.

2.3  MPAS -- Los Alamos National Security, LLC and UCAR  (the rw_mpas_* binaries)

The five rw_mpas_* executables in this bundle -- mesh, static, init, convert,
lbc -- are built from tools/rustwx/crates/rw-mpas, a Rust port of parts of
MPAS-Atmosphere v8.4.1: init_atm_case_lbc, the vertical and horizontal
interpolation of mpas_init_atm_vinterp.F and mpas_init_atm_hinterp.F, the
gravity-wave-drag fields of mpas_init_atm_gwd.F, the deformation and advection
weights of mpas_atm_advection.F and core_sw/mpas_sw_advection.F, mpas_in_cell
from mpas_geometry_utils.F, the constants of mpas_constants.F, and the soil
and surface companions of mpas_atmphys_initialize_real.F.  static_fields, also
in this bundle, is linked by those binaries.

MPAS's licence requires this text to ship with the binary form, and its
government-rights paragraph requires a modified derivative to be clearly
marked: this port is such a derivative and is named rw_mpas, not MPAS.

Copyright (c) 2013-2019,  Los Alamos National Security, LLC (LANS) (Ocean: LA-CC-13-047;
Land Ice: LA-CC-13-117) and the University Corporation for Atmospheric Research (UCAR).

All rights reserved.

DE-AC52-06NA25396 with the U.S. Department of Energy.  UCAR manages the National

2.4  NumPy -- NumPy Developers  (the float32 kernels inside static_fields)

The static_fields library in this bundle contains
tools/rustwx/crates/static-fields/src/projection/npmath.rs, which ports four
of NumPy's own float32 SIMD kernels -- sin, cos, exp and log -- from
numpy/_core/src/umath/loops_trigonometric.dispatch.cpp and
numpy/_core/src/umath/loops_exponent_log.dispatch.c.src at v2.2.6, with
coefficients from npy_simd_data.h.  Same constants, same fused-multiply-add
sequence: the port's contract is byte parity with NumPy.  The rw_mpas_*
executables link that library, so this notice covers them too.

The text below is numpy/LICENSE.txt at v2.2.6, the vintage ported.  Current
upstream reads "Copyright (c) 2005-2025, NumPy Developers." -- a year roll and
nothing else.

Copyright (c) 2005-2024, NumPy Developers.
All rights reserved.

Redistribution and use in source and binary forms, with or without
modification, are permitted provided that the following conditions are
met:

    * Redistributions of source code must retain the above copyright
       notice, this list of conditions and the following disclaimer.

    * Redistributions in binary form must reproduce the above
       copyright notice, this list of conditions and the following
       disclaimer in the documentation and/or other materials provided
       with the distribution.

    * Neither the name of the NumPy Developers nor the names of any
       contributors may be used to endorse or promote products derived
       from this software without specific prior written permission.

THIS SOFTWARE IS PROVIDED BY THE COPYRIGHT HOLDERS AND CONTRIBUTORS
"AS IS" AND ANY EXPRESS OR IMPLIED WARRANTIES, INCLUDING, BUT NOT
LIMITED TO, THE IMPLIED WARRANTIES OF MERCHANTABILITY AND FITNESS FOR
A PARTICULAR PURPOSE ARE DISCLAIMED. IN NO EVENT SHALL THE COPYRIGHT
OWNER OR CONTRIBUTORS BE LIABLE FOR ANY DIRECT, INDIRECT, INCIDENTAL,

and they are listed again at the end of this section.

one copyleft token anywhere in it is r-efi's disjunctive
"MIT OR Apache-2.0 OR LGPL-2.1-or-later"; MIT is elected and the copyleft
branch is not taken.

For the four third-party names in that list -- puffin (Embark Studios),
r-efi, netcdf-reader and hdf5-reader (roteiro-gis,
https://github.com/roteiro-gis/netcdf-rust) -- the grant stated beside the
name is the whole of what upstream declared; the text was not carried into
the vendored tree, and restoring it is a re-vendor operation because six of
those eight directories carry a .cargo-checksum.json that writing into them
would invalidate.

that no distinct copyright line is lost.  Each is headed by the crates that
carried it.
